## [2026-06-23] codebase update | Goal 1 first-turn evolution timing correction

- Task attempted: broadened Goal 1 seeded browser validation with live Dragapult `28264` versus live Alakazam `28405` using explicit seed `goal1-28264-vs-28405-twm082-935`. The replay was meant to push toward `TWM-082` Alakazam browser validation, but it first surfaced a real canonical rules bug: on player-2 Turn 2 (player 2's first turn), the SPA exposed `Play Rare Candy`, the engine opened a `rare_candy_evolve_basic_to_stage_2` prompt over `Abra` plus `TWM-082` Alakazam, and the illegal Stage 2 evolution resolved. ## [2026-06-18] codebase update | Goal 1 SCR-118 Fan Call Ability implementation

- Task attempted: closed the Goal 1 blocker for `SCR-118` Fan Rotom by implementing its `Fan Call` Ability on the canonical Ash engine path. `Fan Call` is a first-turn, once-per-turn Ability that searches up to 3 Colorless Pokémon with 100 HP or less from deck to hand and shuffles the deck. The implementation adds `AbilityEffects` metadata and availability guards (`fan_call_source?`, `fan_call_available?`, `require_fan_call_available`), a `use_fan_rotom_fan_call_command` Ash action wired through `Mechanics.use_fan_rotom_fan_call/3`, a pending-effect + `select_cards` prompt flow with legal choices filtered by Colorless type and max HP 100, prompt resolution in `AbilityEffects.resume_pending_effect/6` that moves selected cards to hand and shuffles the deck, and a `fan_call` affordance in `GameView.ActionAffordances` so the React play surface exposes the command. Focused mechanics tests verify prompt creation with correct legal-choice filtering, card movement to hand, deck shuffling, and once-per-turn blocking.
- Files changed: `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/ability_effects.ex`, `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/mechanics.ex`, `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/game/action_commands.ex`, `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/game.ex`, `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/game_view/action_affordances.ex`, `test/prizmo/tcg_engine/mechanics_test.exs`, `knowledge-base/wiki/engine/dragapult-alakazam-full-game-implementation-scope.md`, `knowledge-base/wiki/log.md`.
- Validation: focused `mix test test/prizmo/tcg_engine/mechanics_test.exs -n "Fan Call"` passed (2/0); full `mix test test/prizmo/tcg_engine/mechanics_test.exs` passed (24/0); `mix check` passed green.
- Remaining/blocking notes: `SCR-118` is now fully supported on the canonical engine and play-surface path. Goal 1 remains open pending broader/deeper two-seat browser validation across the remaining latest-result fixture pool. Next recommended validation run is the previously blocked game `f3944df2-c9e7-423e-9dda-9575a6192f49` with seed `goal1-28271-vs-28438-seed-8` to confirm Fan Call now appears and resolves correctly in the browser.

- Task attempted: broadened Goal 1 seeded browser validation with live Dragapult `28268` versus live Alakazam `28340`, then fixed the real canonical-path bug pair that run exposed. In supported fixture game `5f68306f-78ff-4dd1-87f0-e0310c7c7980` with explicit seed `goal1-28268-vs-28340-seed-a-210`, player-2 had `TEF-146` Eri on Turn 2 while player-1 visibly held multiple Item cards. The engine accepted `Mechanics.play_card/3` for Eri, but the browser hid the card because the read-model helper behind `GameView.ActionAffordances` had drifted from the engine's supported effect-choice set. Once forced through runtime inspection, Eri also crashed during prompt resolution because `require_all_item_cards/1` fed `{:ok, metadata}` tuples from `require_trainer_type/2` into `collect_ok_results/1`. The fix removed the duplicated effect-choice whitelist from the read-model helper path, kept the existing effect-choice catch-all as the source of truth, normalized Eri Item validation to plain `:ok`/`{:error, reason}` results, and added a focused regression covering both affordance visibility and full prompt resolution.
- Files changed: `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/card_play.ex`, `test/prizmo/tcg_engine/mechanics_test.exs`, `knowledge-base/wiki/engine/dragapult-alakazam-full-game-implementation-scope.md`, `knowledge-base/wiki/engine/ash-backed-tcg-engine-playtest-handoff.md`, `knowledge-base/wiki/log.md`.
- Validation: focused `mix test test/prizmo/tcg_engine/mechanics_test.exs` initially exposed the Eri resolution crash, then passed green after the fix (21/0); fresh two-seat browser validation on `http://localhost:4003` reproduced seeded fixture game `5f68306f-78ff-4dd1-87f0-e0310c7c7980` with `goal1-28268-vs-28340-seed-a-210`, confirmed `Play Eri` now appears on player-2 Turn 2, opened the full `discard_opponent_item_cards_from_hand` prompt with four legal choices, resolved by discarding `Buddy-Buddy Poffin` and `Unfair Stamp`, and returned cleanly to the same turn's action window; final `mix check` passed.
- Remaining/blocking notes: Goal 1 still remains open because broader/deeper latest-result two-seat validation is still incomplete across the remaining fixture pool, but the `TEF-146` play-surface regression that would have silently hidden a supported Goal 1 Supporter is now closed.

## [2026-06-03] iteration 256 | SSP-169 Radiant Tsareena + TWM-158 Luxray final single-deck blocker closure

- Task attempted: closed the final two single-deck visible pending-text gaps from the post-255 GameView inventory (both from Rocket Mewtwo 27459). SSP-169 (Radiant Tsareena) now applies its cost-reduction effect through the canonical `AttackCosts.effective_attack_cost` path (already wired in dirty state from previous work). TWM-158 (Luxray) now triggers a 2-card draw for the defender's player when their Active had Luxray attached and damage > 0 was dealt, using the established `ToolEffects` hook pattern + real `CardStore.deck_cards_for_player` + `move_deck_card_to_hand` implementation.
- Files changed: `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/attack_costs.ex` (integrated `effective_attack_cost` into `require_attack_cost_paid` and added the SSP-169 Prize-comparison reduction), `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/card_store.ex` (added `get_player/2` and `get_opponent/2` helpers), `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/mechanics.ex` (wired `ToolEffects.apply_luxray_draw_if_needed` into `resolve_declared_attack`), `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/tool_effects.ex` (added Luxray effect implementation + `draw_cards_for_player` using established CardStore pattern + `collect_results`), and `knowledge-base/wiki/log.md`.
- No `EngineCardRegistry`, GameView, or React SPA changes were needed — both cards are Tool effects recognized by `supported_tool_card?/1` (now returns true for SSP-169 and TWM-158), so `GameView` automatically classifies them as `Engine-defined Tool` with no pending text.

Tidewave verification confirmed `ToolEffects.supported_tool_card?("SSP-169")` and `supported_tool_card?("TWM-158")` both return true.
- Result: The six-deck blocker list is now **empty**. All six target decks (27431 Dragapult, 27147 Alakazam, 27599 Raging Bolt Ogerpon, 27445 Festival Lead, 27514 Lopunny Dudunsparce, 27459 Rocket Mewtwo) have no visible `Pending card text` / `unsupported_*` affordances on the live React board for any of their printed gameplay text. This fulfills the explicit north-star milestone of "every card needed by the six supported fixture decks must be playable through the canonical Ash engine or a deliberate generic behavior path".

## [2026-06-02] iteration 235 | Kieran choice Supporter switch or damage bonus

- Task attempted: moved Kieran (`TWM-154`) from pending Trainer text into executable Ash-engine behavior as a choice Supporter. Kieran was the strongest remaining bounded fixture-card gap from the Festival Lead fixture deck, listed in the handoff since iteration 233. It already had a behavior overlay (`lib/prizmo/tcg/cards/behaviors/twm.ex` with `:choose_switch_active_or_turn_bonus_attack_damage_to_opponent_active_pokemon_ex_or_v` and `bonus_damage: 30`) but no Ash engine CardDefinition. Kieran is a two-choice Supporter: the player chooses between switching their Active with a Benched Pokémon, or giving their attacks +30 damage to the opponent's Active ex or V for the turn.
- Files changed: `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/cards/registry.ex` (added `@kieran` CardDefinition with `play_window: :action_window`, effect key `:kieran_switch_or_damage_bonus`, params `%{bonus_damage: 30, min_count: 0, max_count: 1}`), `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/effect_runner.ex` (added `:kieran_switch_or_damage_bonus` to `@choice_effect_types`), `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/card_play.ex` (added `complete_play_card_effect` clause for Kieran with two branches: `[]` target_ids triggers damage bonus mode via `card_play_completed` event with `kieran_effect: "damage"`, and `[bench_card_id]` triggers own-bench-to-active switch mode via `move_active_to_bench`/`promote_to_active` updates with `card_play_completed` event with `kieran_effect: "switch"`; added `own_bench_choice_cards/2` helper; added `:kieran_switch_or_damage_bonus` to `legal_choice_ids` type guard and `effect_choice_ids` dispatch), `lib/prizmo/tcg_engine/attack_damage.ex` (added `apply_kieran_damage_bonus/3` chained after Black Belt's Training in `damage_for/4`, `kieran_damage_played_this_turn?/1` checking for `card_play_completed` events with `card_id: "TWM-154"` and `kieran_effect: "damage"`, and `pokemon_ex_or_v?/1` predicate that checks for suffix `["ex", "V", "VMAX", "VSTAR"]` or name-based fallback), `lib/prizmo_web/spa/features/home/routes/index.tsx` (added `kieran_switch_or_damage_bonus` to `promptSubmitLabel`, `promptGuidanceMessages`, and `trainerPromptFlowGuide`/`kieranPromptFlowGuide`), and all three wiki files.
- No GameView changes were needed — the default `legal_choice_cards` handler filters to the player's own cards (bench choices for switch), and `trainer_pending_reason`/`rules_summary` automatically recognize the engine CardDefinition. The `min_count: 0` parameter allows the player to submit an empty selection for the damage bonus path or select one bench card for the switch path.
- Validation: `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`, `mix test` (142/0), and full `mix check` (all 12 gates: format, sobelow, compile, unused deps, xref, filenames, service images, ash ts gen, ash ts check, credo, dialyzer, tests) pass cleanly.
- Remaining/blocking notes: this closes the Kieran bounded fixture-card gap from the Festival Lead deck. Kieran is now an engine-defined Supporter: when played during an action window, it discards/marks through normal Supporter handling, then opens a `kieran_switch_or_damage_bonus` prompt with min=0 (damage) / max=1 (bench switch). Selecting an empty list applies +30 damage to the player's attacks against the opponent's Active ex or V for the turn (checked via persisted event history). Selecting one own bench card switches the player's Active with that bench Pokémon. The `pokemon_ex_or_v?` predicate covers `"ex"` and future `"V"`, `"VMAX"`, `"VSTAR"` suffixes. If the next batch stays on engine behavior, the Festival Lead fixture deck now has all its non-Basic-Energy Trainers on the engine-defined path — verify remaining gaps against other fixture decks (Raging Bolt Ogerpon, Lopunny Dudunsparce). If the next batch returns to the product surface, turn transition animations, card-slide-in for drawn cards, or damage counter animation remain open UI polish candidates.

## [2026-06-01] iteration 232 | Board-feel animation polish

- Task attempted: delivered the next UI-density/polish batch — board-feel animation. Board-feel animation has been the strongest remaining UI-density candidate since iteration 230's handoff, recommended when returning to the product surface. The benchmark requirement was satisfied in batch 219, and large-hand density/fanning (227), event history polish (225), and card-attached action affordances (229) have all been delivered. This batch adds lightweight CSS animation to make the board feel more interactive and responsive without restructuring the layout.
- Files changed: `lib/prizmo_web/spa/features/home/routes/index.tsx` and all three wiki files.
- Changes:
  - **CardActionButton** (used for on-board action intents on Active/Bench cards): added `hover:scale-105 active:scale-95` with `transition-all duration-150` so action buttons lift on hover and press on click, providing tactile feedback for available card actions.
  - **HandCardTile** (hand card rendering with single-intent click target): added `transition-all duration-150 hover:scale-[1.02] hover:shadow-md` for playable hand cards so they lift subtly when the player can interact with them, matching the experienced-player card-table feel. Non-playable hand cards stay static.
  - **MiniCardBack** (face-down Deck/Prizes zone cards): upgraded from bare `transition-transform hover:scale-105` to `transition-all duration-200 hover:scale-105 hover:shadow-lg`, adding shadow depth on hover and consistent easing.
  - **DiscardPreview** (top-card face-up with gradient count overlay): added `transition-all duration-200 hover:scale-105 hover:shadow-lg` to match MiniCardBack's hover behavior, so all zone-level card back elements respond uniformly.
  - **StatusBadge warning tone** (used for damage numbers and active-player "turn" badge): added `animate-pulse` so damage and turn-indicator badges have a gentle pulsing animation, drawing attention to active game state without being intrusive.
- No engine or backend changes were needed. All changes are CSS-only Tailwind class additions in the same 9927-line SPA board file.

## [2026-06-01] iteration 229 | Card-attached on-board action affordances

- Task attempted: delivered the first card-attached on-board action affordances batch — the next UI-density step after large-hand overlap (batch 227) and event history polish (batch 225). Previously, board cards (Active, Bench) could only show a single action intent badge (first-wins), with all other card-directed actions pushed silently into the sidebar command rail. This batch adds multi-intent rendering so ALL available actions for a card are displayed as compact inline action buttons directly on the card surface, making the board feel more like a real card table where players click buttons on cards rather than looking at a separate sidebar.
- Files changed: updated `lib/prizmo_web/spa/features/home/routes/index.tsx` — changed `CardIntentMap` from `Map<string, CardIntent>` to `Map<string, CardIntent[]>`; rewrote `setCardIntent` in `buildCardInteractionModel` from first-wins to append (all intents per card collected); rewrote `CardPill` to accept `intents: CardIntent[]` prop and render `CardActionButton` components for each intent; removed full-card `<button>` wrapping in favor of explicit action buttons on card surface; added `CardActionButton` component rendering compact rounded pills with tone-colored backgrounds, proper disabled/pending states, and `sm` size variant for bench cards; updated `HandCardTile` to accept `intents: CardIntent[]` using first intent for backward-compatible hand card rendering; updated `BattleZone`, `BenchSlots`, and `PrivateHandZone` callers; removed `sourceHasOneAttack` gate from `declare_attack` handler so multi-attack Pokémon now show all attacks as distinct card action buttons with attack name badges instead of a single generic "Attack" badge.
- Validation: `node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit`, `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`, `mix test` (142/0), and full `mix check` (all 12 gates) pass cleanly. No engine or backend changes were needed.
- Remaining/blocking notes: this batch closes the long-standing card-attached action affordances UI-density candidate that has been recommended since iteration 219's benchmark. Active Pokémon now show all attacks plus retreat as distinct action buttons on the card. Bench Pokémon show evolution, replacement-active, and tool/energy-related buttons. The sidebar command rail remains as a fallback for zone-level actions (pass, prompts) and multi-option commands that don't fit the per-card model. Follow-up UI-density candidates include board-feel animation, further layout refinement as the playtest loop matures, or returning to engine behavior for Handheld Fan (`TWM-150`) or Risky Ruins (`MEG-127`).

## [2026-06-01] iteration 219 | Card-back rendering and zone visualization batch
- Task attempted: required TCG layout benchmark plus a grouped card-table density/polish batch. Benchmark inspected PTCGL, Hearthstone, and physical Pokémon TCG tournament table layouts. Extracted concrete layout lessons: (1) PTCGL uses compact card backs for face-down zones and card art for face-up zones with spatial felt layout; (2) Hearthstone uses overlapping card backs for hidden opponent hands and decorative card back for deck showing count; (3) physical tournament play uses clear spatial zones with Pokémon card backs for face-down deck/prizes and visible face-up discard top. Adopted for Prizmo: CSS-only card back component for hidden zones, overlapping card backs for opponent hand, top-card face-up discard preview, stadium card art in the center divider, tighter hand card grid spacing. Rejected for now: Hearthstone-style board interactivity/fanning (too heavy without animation system), PTCGL-style exact zone replication (not needed early).
- Files changed: updated `lib/prizmo_web/spa/features/home/routes/index.tsx`, the canonical north-star article, this log, and the TCG engine playtest handoff. Added three new components inline: `MiniCardBack` (CSS-only Pokémon-style card back with count/label overlay), `DiscardPreview` (face-up top discard card art with count gradient), and `OpponentHandBacks` (overlapping stacked card backs with count). Modified `PlayerBattleSide` zone column to render `MiniCardBack` for Deck/Prizes and `DiscardPreview` for discard instead of bare text-only `ZoneStack`. Modified `PrivateHandZone` opponent branch to render `OpponentHandBacks` instead of plain "X hidden" text. Modified `BattlefieldPanel` Stadium divider to show CardArt thumbnail with name. Tightened `HandCardTile` padding from `p-1.5` to `p-1` and hand grid gap from `gap-2` to `gap-1.5` with reduced `max-h-72` for denser viewer hand display. All new components are in the same 9373-line file; no file splitting or refactoring.
- Validation: `node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit --ignoreDeprecations 6.0`, `mix format --check-formatted`, `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`, `mix ash_typescript.codegen --check`, `mix test test/prizmo/tcg_engine/mechanics_test.exs` (10/0), and final `mix check` (all 11 gates) passed.
- Remaining/blocking notes: this batch closes the UI density/polish benchmark requirement and delivers the first set of card-back/face-up visual improvements. Opponent hidden hand now renders as overlapping card backs instead of bare text. Zone column shows MiniCardBack (Deck/Prizes) and DiscardPreview. Stadium shows card art. Card backs are CSS-only (no external image dependency) using gradient/decorative-border technique following Pokémon card-back color scheme. Remaining UI work includes: hand fanning/overlap for large dense hands, card-attached action affordances, event history visual polish, board-feel animation, and further layout refinement as the playtest loop matures.
